## statement
Exact fixed-b=3 benchmark thresholds and conditional full-certificate amplification for five- and six-step seeds.

For a nonnegative integer n and a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over all finite-dimensional Euclidean spaces, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, under x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0 is arbitrary and fixed; the empty schedule is allowed. This is the W=2R normalization, where R is the same supremum without the factor2.

For nonnegative real a,d define

    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain the ORIGINAL ALL-split benchmark recurrences

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),  integer n>=1,
    U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2).

Every split index k is an integer. For every positive integer b let Z_b be the positive finite limit lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), established in predecessor04219554e41d9750. Define

    Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000,
    Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000,

and let Gamma_r_c107 denote Gamma5_c107 for r=5 and Gamma6_c107 for r=6. With the denominator ray FIXED at b=3, the strict comparisons are

    Z_18/Z_3 < Gamma5_c107 < 99/10 < 292666/29275,
    Z_21/Z_3 < Gamma6_c107 < 1201/100 < 90/7.

The following assertions are conditional. Fix r in {5,6}. Suppose h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) is an ACTUAL nonnegative seed word with s=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)h_i and capacity A=1+s>Gamma_r_c107. Suppose there is a full real (r+1)-by-(r+1) array Lambda with Lambda_ii=0 and Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in {0,...,r}. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1; let the real matrix T have T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i. Set

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

Require

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2
        positive semidefinite on the entire R^(r+1).

Here diag of a column is the diagonal matrix with that column, and a superscript T on a matrix or column means transpose. Thus r=5 requires all30 ordered multipliers and all6 formal gradients, and r=6 requires all42 multipliers and all7 formal gradients. A zero physical step permits neither deletion nor equality of formal gradient coordinates. No rank, support, symmetry, or zero-remainder restriction is imposed.

For each nonnegative integer n choose ANY fully expanded maximizing tree of the original B_n recurrence, expanding every C child by the original C recurrence. Replace each of its n primitive empty leaves by the actual word h, retain every internal node, and leave its one terminal dominant empty leaf EMPTY with mass1. Recompute each physical bridge as follows. A primitive join, with actual child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right), uses the unique positive d_pp solving d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right and concatenates (left,1+d_pp,right). A dominant join, with primitive left capacity A_left and actual dominant right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), uses zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2 and concatenates (left,1+A_left/zeta,right). Write Hsub_n for this actual physical word and Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n for its mass.

For every n>=0 the accepted full substitution identities give

    length(Hsub_n)=(r+1)n,
    W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,
    ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).

The equality is an all-function objective upper certificate with a matching one-dimensional Huber lower witness. For every nonnegative integer j put

    n_j=3*2^j-1,
    N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1),
    q107_j=(2/3)^j,
    epsilon_c107=1-Gamma_r_c107/A in (0,1).

At these TRUE physical horizons,

    lim_(j->infinity) B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
        =Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3)<1.

For every j with q107_j<1/3 one has the finite bound

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
        < (Gamma_r_c107/A)/(1-3q107_j).

In particular, for EVERY integer

    j>=ceil(log(6/epsilon_c107)/log(3/2)),

where log is the natural logarithm,

    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-epsilon_c107/2) U_(N_j)^ZJ.

Each of the following separate sufficient hypotheses gives the uniform strict bound W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/16000)U_(N_j)^ZJ for EVERY integer j>=27:

    r=5 and A>=99/10, with N_j=18*2^j-6;
    r=6 and A>=1201/100, with N_j=21*2^j-7.

This theorem supplies sufficient upper enclosures for this fixed ray and is conditional on an actual full seed. It supplies no such seed, proves no seed nonexistence, and asserts no global phase optimum, no ordering between the TRUE b=1 and b=3 ratios, and no unrestricted all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile theorem. The last two comparisons to292666/29275 and90/7 are numerical comparisons of displayed constants, not an invocation or acceptance of another seed-threshold draft.

## proof
# Exact fixed-integer-ray thresholds for five- and six-step seeds

The sole mathematical predecessor is fact04219554e41d9750, specifically
its proof Sections2--3 and7--13. Every reference below to the accepted
fact or an accepted section means that predecessor. Its general-length
full-seed substitution and saved endpoint are used in their proved scope.
All new scalar comparisons and constant checks are proved jointly below.
No unverified finding or analytical audit is a mathematical premise.

## 1. Statement and original benchmark

For a nonnegative integer n and a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h)
is the supremum of

  2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite Euclidean dimension, every convex differentiable f
with L-Lipschitz gradient and a minimizer x_star, and x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0; this is W=2R.

For a,d>=0 put

  Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
  G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain the ORIGINAL ALL-split recurrences

  C_0=B_0=1,
  C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
  B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
  U_n^ZJ=1/B_n,   lambda=1+sqrt(2),   rho=log_2(lambda).

The accepted fact defines the positive finite limits

  Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),  integer b>=1.

Freeze b=3 for BOTH lengths before making any limiting comparison. Define

  Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000,
  Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000.

For r in {5,6}, Gamma_r_c107 means the corresponding displayed constant.
Every dyadic depth j below is a nonnegative integer.

The new strict sufficient bounds are

  Z_18/Z_3 < Gamma5_c107 < 99/10 < 292666/29275,             (1)
  Z_21/Z_3 < Gamma6_c107 < 1201/100 < 90/7.                (2)

Consequently, CONDITIONAL on an actual full-certified primitive seed
of length r in {5,6} and capacity A=1+sum h>Gamma_r_c107, the original
substitution from the accepted fact, with n_j=3*2^j-1, has actual horizon

  N_j=(r+1)(3*2^j-1).

Let epsilon_c107=1-Gamma_r_c107/A, so 0<epsilon_c107<1. For EVERY integer

  j >= ceil(log(6/epsilon_c107)/log(3/2)),                  (3)

the substituted physical word satisfies

  W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-epsilon_c107/2) U_(N_j)^ZJ.    (4)

In particular each of the following separate sufficient conditions gives
the uniform strict margin 1/16000 for EVERY j>=27:

  r=5, A>=99/10,      N_j=18*2^j-6;
  r=6, A>=1201/100,   N_j=21*2^j-7.                      (5)

The full seed certificate meant here is stated in Section6. The constants
are upper enclosures, not a lower barrier or a global optimum over rays.
In particular (1)--(2) compare sufficient bounds; they do NOT establish
that the true ratios at b=3 are below the true ratios at b=1.

## 2. All-split comparison with primitive capacities

We prove jointly that

  G(a,lambda d)<=lambda Omega(a,d)   for all a,d>=0.       (6)

When a=d=0 both sides are zero. Otherwise put w=Omega(a,d)>0 and t=a/w.
The equation w^2-(a+d)w-ad=0 gives

  0<=t<=1,    d/w=(1-t)/(1+t).

The larger root of P(v)=v^2-(4a+lambda d)v+4a^2 is G(a,lambda d).
Since lambda^2=2lambda+1, substitution and expansion give

  P(lambda w)/w^2
    =lambda^2-4lambda t-lambda^2*(1-t)/(1+t)+4t^2
    =4t*(t-1/sqrt(2))^2/(1+t) >=0.

Also w>=a+d, so lambda w>=lambda(a+d)>=2a+lambda d/2,
the vertex of P. A quadratic of positive leading coefficient is
nonnegative to the right of its vertex only at or beyond its larger
root. This proves (6), including either zero argument.

Both kernels are increasing in each nonnegative argument directly from
their displayed formulas. Since B_0=1<=lambda C_0, induction over every
ORIGINAL split k+l=n-1 gives

  G(C_k,B_l)<=G(C_k,lambda C_l)
             <=lambda Omega(C_k,C_l)<=lambda C_n.

Taking the maximum over all splits proves B_n<=lambda C_n. The accepted
symmetric identity C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1) therefore implies

  Z_b<=lambda C_(b-1),  for every fixed integer b>=1.      (7)

No optimizing split for B_n has been selected or discarded.

## 3. Exact discrete primitive identities and rational enclosures

The accepted fact's Section2 gives the balanced identities for U_N=C_(N-1),
with U_0=0:

  U_(2a)=lambda U_a,
  U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)).

They are statements about the symmetric ALL-split recurrence, not a
balanced assumption about B. Set the following new local quantities:

  c107=C_2=Omega(1,lambda),
  p107=C_4=Omega(lambda,c107),
  u107=C_8=Omega(lambda^2,p107),
  v107=C_10=Omega(p107,lambda*c107),
  w107=C_20=Omega(lambda*p107,v107).

The identities also give C_5=lambda*c107, C_9=lambda*p107, and
C_17=lambda*u107. Thus (7) yields

  Z_18<=lambda^2*u107,     Z_21<=lambda*w107.              (8)

Here are complete rational upper checks. First

  12/5<lambda<169/70,   lambda^2=2lambda+1<204/35,

because (7/5)^2<2<(99/70)^2. For positive a,d define

  P_(a,d)(x)=x^2-(a+d)x-ad.

Its positive root is Omega(a,d). Each proposed x in the table below
exceeds a+d; hence P_(a,d)(x)>0 proves x>Omega(a,d).

quantity    a                 d                  x          P_(a,d)(x)
c107        1                 169/70             502/125    2405/1093750
p107        169/70            502/125            1923/250   4084/437500
u107        204/35            1923/250           407/25     3943/43750
v107        1923/250          42419/4375         419/20     854258/17500000
w107        324987/17500      419/20             23841/500  804318/8750000

The exact substitutions used in the last two rows are

  lambda*c107 < (169/70)(502/125)=42419/4375,
  lambda*p107 < (169/70)(1923/250)=324987/17500.

For an explicit audit of the larger rows, the u107 numerator on
denominator43750 is

  11595430-9630027-1961460=3943.

The v107 numerator on denominator17500000 is

  7680793750-6374791700-1305147792=854258.

The w107 numerator on denominator8750000 is

  19893764835-16488721692-3404238825=804318.

All entries are strictly positive. Monotonicity and the preceding exact
identities prove, in particular,

  u107<407/25,     w107<23841/500.                         (9)

We will also need c107>4. The polynomial defining c107 has value
16-4(1+lambda)-lambda=12-5lambda<0 at4, so its positive root exceeds4.

## 4. The accepted finite endpoint gives the denominator

Use ONLY the saved endpoint in accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections8--9:

  D80=1208925819614629174706176,
  L3071=77954524439662110401113475160,
  B_3071>=L3071/D80,
  L3071-64000D80=583271984325843219918211160.

No part of the defining DP or certificate check is rerun. Subtracting the
displayed integer480D80 gives the purely textual comparison

  L3071-64480D80=2987590910821216059246680>0.               (10)

The accepted lower ray enclosure at b=3,j=10 has finite index3071,
so

  Z_3>=lambda^(-10) B_3071
     >=lambda^(-10) L3071/D80
      >64480/lambda^10.                                  (11)

The elementary power identities

  lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2),
  lambda^6=99+70sqrt(2),
  lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2),
  lambda^12=19601+13860sqrt(2)

follow by multiplication using lambda^2=2lambda+1. Since the conjugates
of the even powers are their positive reciprocals,

  lambda^10+lambda^(-10)=6726,
  lambda^12+lambda^(-12)=39202.

It follows that

  lambda^10<6726,
  lambda^12<39202,
  lambda^11<6726*(169/70)=1136694/70<16239.               (12)

Equations (8)--(12) now give

  Z_18/Z_3 < lambda^12*u107/64480
            < 39202*(407/25)/64480
             =7977607/806000=Gamma5_c107,

  Z_21/Z_3 < lambda^11*w107/64480
            < 16239*(23841/500)/64480
             =387153999/32240000=Gamma6_c107.             (13)

The strict comparisons to the convenient capacities are

  99/10-Gamma5_c107=1793/806000>0,
  1201/100-Gamma6_c107=48401/32240000>0.

The improvement over the previously sufficient b=1 bounds needs no
assumption that those unverified proofs are accepted:

  292666/29275-99/10=28435/292750>0,
  90/7-1201/100=593/700>0.

This proves (1)--(2) as one joint argument from the accepted input.

## 5. Quantitative approach to the fixed denominator ray

The accepted Section9 gives, with n_j=3*2^j-1,

  0<=Z_3-lambda^(-j)B_(n_j)<=E(3,j),

  E(3,j)=8sqrt(2)*3^(rho-1)*2^(-j)
          +(3/25)*4^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)
          +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).

Define q107_j=(2/3)^j. The accepted bound1<rho<4/3 implies
3^(rho-1)<3/2, since3<(3/2)^3. Also sqrt(2)<3/2,
4^(rho/2)=lambda<5/2, and sqrt(lambda)>3/2. Consequently

  E(3,j)
    <18*2^(-j)+(3/10)*lambda^(-j/2)+(1/1000)*lambda^(-j)
    <=(18+3/10+1/1000)q107_j<19q107_j.                   (14)

Equation (11) and lambda^10<6726 give

  Z_3>64480/6726>19/2,

where the last comparison is128960>127794. Hence

  B_(n_j)>lambda^j Z_3(1-2q107_j).                       (15)

The accepted lower comparison B_n>=C_n and the exact symmetric identity
give the second useful estimate

  B_(n_j)>=C_(n_j)=lambda^j*c107>4lambda^j.              (16)

Neither estimate evaluates a new B value.

## 6. Actual seed requirement and the physical output

Fix r in {5,6}. The hypothesis is an ACTUAL nonnegative word
h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)), s=sum h_i and A=1+s>Gamma_r_c107, together with
nonnegative real Lambda_ij for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in{0,...,r}
and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1 and set

  T_ii=1,   T_it=h_t for t<i,   T_it=0 for t>i,
  L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
  omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
  D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

Require the FULL original-coordinate conditions

  L_flow^T e=omega,
  M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).

This means30 multipliers and all6 formal gradients for r=5, or42
multipliers and all7 formal gradients for r=6. A zero physical step
does not permit deletion or equality of its formal gradient coordinate.
No rank, support, symmetry or remainder-zero condition is imposed.

Accepted04219554e41d9750 Sections10--13 applies for these general lengths.
For each n choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the ORIGINAL
B_n recurrence. Replace its n primitive empty leaves by this actual h;
leave its single terminal dominant empty leaf empty. Retain all internal
nodes and recompute the physical bridges specified in that accepted fact:

* A primitive join with capacities A_left,A_right uses bridge1+d_pp,
  where d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right.
* A dominant join with primitive capacity A_left and right mass D_right
  uses bridge1+A_left/zeta, where
  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

Use the accepted names Hsub_n for the resulting word and Dsub_n for
its mass1+2sum Hsub_n. Its conclusions, including payment for the
untouched dominant leaf and the Huber lower witness, are

  length(Hsub_n)=(r+1)n,
  W_((r+1)n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
  Dsub_n>=A B_n(1-ell_n)^2,
  ell_n=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_n).

These are cited accepted results, not new composition claims.

For n=n_j, (16) gives 0<=ell_(n_j)<q107_j/2. Also

  N_j=(r+1)n_j=3(r+1)2^j-(r+1).

By monotonicity of B and its accepted lower ray enclosure at
b=3(r+1),

  B_(N_j)<=B_(3(r+1)2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(3(r+1)).          (17)

Thus the finite output is compared at its TRUE horizon; no offset is
silently removed. For q107_j<1/3, (15)--(17) and (13) give

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
     < (Gamma_r_c107/A)
        /[(1-2q107_j)(1-q107_j/2)^2]
     <=(Gamma_r_c107/A)/(1-3q107_j),                     (18)

because

  (1-2q)(1-q/2)^2=1-3q+(9/4)q^2-(1/2)q^3>=1-3q

for0<=q<1/3. The already accepted shifted-ray statement also gives the
exact conditional limiting ratio

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) -> Z_(3(r+1))/(A Z_3),

with the same finite offset. The finite bound (18) is stronger than an
unspecified eventuality.

If q107_j<=epsilon_c107/6, (18) implies

  B_(N_j) W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j))
    <(1-epsilon_c107)/(1-epsilon_c107/2)
    <1-epsilon_c107/2.

The final strict inequality follows by subtracting: the square
(1-epsilon_c107/2)^2 exceeds1-epsilon_c107 byepsilon_c107^2/4.
Condition (3) ensures exactly q107_j<=epsilon_c107/6, proving (4).

For the convenient capacities in (5), the respective lower margins are

  1-Gamma5_c107/(99/10)=1793/7979400>1/8000,
  1-Gamma6_c107/(1201/100)=48401/387202400>1/8000.

For the second strict comparison,
48401*8000-387202400=5600>0; the first follows from
1793*8000=14344000>7979400. Increasing A only increases epsilon_c107.
Finally

  (3/2)^27=((3/2)^10)^2*(3/2)^7
           >57^2*17=55233>48000,

using59049>57*1024 and2187>17*128. Thus for EVERY j>=27,
q107_j<1/48000<epsilon_c107/6. Equation (4) gives the claimed strict
factor1-1/16000 at the respective actual horizons.

## 7. Scope

No seed existence, all-word lower bound, global ray optimum, or ordering
of the true b=1 and b=3 ratios is inferred. Dyadic rescaling alone is
not a new phase: the definition gives Z_(2b)=lambda Z_b, hence preserves
Z_((r+1)b)/Z_b. Nothing here asserts density or continuity of phases.

The conditional consequence requires an actual seed satisfying Section6.
No pointwise domination claim about another seed family is a premise.
This theorem does not supply such a seed, prove its nonexistence, or
settle the original all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile problem.

## intuition
A comparison of the original all-split dominant recurrence with the primitive capacities, together with the accepted fixed3071 lower endpoint, gives strict fixed-b3 thresholds. The accepted full physical substitution pays for the untouched dominant leaf, and an explicit convergence estimate gives the true-horizon finite margins. Actual seed existence remains a hypothesis.

fact_id: 5b7ff4253c38b90a

predecessors: [04219554e41d9750]
glossary_introduces:
  Aword_i: Actual chronological ordinary block incoming to selected checkpoint i; Aword_k is the actual terminal ordinary block.
  C_eff: Effective limiting coefficient m*log chi, equal to 7*pi/5.
  C_min: Explicit polyhedron (29) equal to the entire minimum set of F_m.
  D_bulk,j: Scalar normalized denominator chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass*(1+xi_j).
  D_first: Scalar normalized denominator chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+xi_J), distinct from the physical scales D_i.
  F_m: Explicit convex1-smooth static half-sum in (21); the L-smooth function is f_m=L*F_m.
  G_i: Actual transfer b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)).
  Hub_delta: One-sided Huber function: 0 on z<=0, z^2/2 on 0<=z<=delta, and delta*z-delta^2/2 on z>=delta.
  K_arc: Convex hull of the origin and the support arc v(u), 0<=u<=theta_sp.
  L1023: Fixed certified saved lower endpoint numerator 19205851554343006584447327614 for B_1023 on the accepted denominator D80.
  L_joint: Exact limiting B_(2^m-1)-scaled payoff Z_1/(2lambda)*exp[-1+(8+4sqrt2-14pi/5-1/50)*(1-5pi/(28log lambda))] of the constructed original-ruler instance.
  P5_m: Different scalar reference (31), with b-1 numerator, maximum activation and terminal1/(1+2s); it is not silently assigned as this function's finite payoff.
  P_i: Sum of local half-step times positive horizontal arc component in the incoming block i.
  P_m: Actual specified-minimizer normalized payoff of the static half-sum function, equal to the exact source-format product (4) in the joint evidence.
  Q_ab: Original ordered interpolation residual f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2 for the normalized 1-smooth function.
  Q_i: Sum of local half-step times nonnegative negative-vertical arc component in the incoming block i.
  R(u): Spiral radius chi*exp(-kappa*u).
  R_cut: The unchanged exact selection budget max{A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2}.
  S_j: Sum of the steps in the original enclosing-m word H_j.
  U_i: Local zero-region horizontal coordinate 1-sigma_i*(P_i+epsilon*b^(i)/2) in Sections 4 onward.
  U_j: In Section 3 only, horizontal coordinate of the backward state r_j=(U_j,-Y_j); this is distinct from a local zero-region constant.
  V_fin: Finite set consisting of 0, v(0), and every selected backward projection v_0,...,v_N.
  V_j: Relative additive activation cost 2*A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j), where s^(J)=S_J and s^(j)=S_(j-1) for j>J.
  Y_i: Local zero-region vertical coordinate sigma_i*Q_i in Sections 4 onward.
  Y_j: In Section 3 only, positive negative-vertical coordinate of r_j; this is distinct from the local zero-region constant.
  Z_1: Positive finite original benchmark ray limit lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1), supplied by predecessor 04219554e41d9750.
  alpha_eff: Effective exponential activation coefficient, equal to 5*pi/28.
  b^(i): The selected first-occurrence level-J+i step b_(J+i), for 0<=i<k.
  b_j: Physical original level-j checkpoint value 1+a*c^j.
  chi: Spiral horizontal allowance sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp).
  d_bulk: Leading normalized later denominator chi+epsilon*v_mass.
  d_first: Leading normalized first denominator chi+epsilon*(c-2).
  delta_l: Exact relative word-mass error [(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l), with zeta=a/(c-2).
  delta_tail: Terminal Huber slope D_k/(1+s_k).
  e(u): Planar radial unit vector (cos u,-sin u).
  ell_loss: Additional limiting transfer loss in Section 12, here zero.
  eta_i: Section12 explicit per-transfer loss factors, all at least1 and with sum log eta_i tending0.
  eta_margin: Positive limit slack (L_joint-1001/1000)/2 used to obtain one eventual cutoff M.
  f_i: Local envelope min_z [phi_i(z)+||x-z||^2/2] for block i; scalar query values are explicitly scoped separately.
  f_loc: Generic local envelope min_z [phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2] in Section 4.
  f_m: The L-smooth objective L*F_m for arbitrary fixed L>0.
  hatD_i: Section12 bookkeeping scales equal to the actual scales except hatD_k=sqrt(tau_tail)*D_k, paying the primary-interface terminal correction.
  n(u): Supporting normal e(u)+kappa*t(u) to the full arc hull.
  n_m: Actual physical horizon 2^m-1 of H_m.
  p(u): Positive horizontal coordinate of v(u).
  phi_i: Nonnegative finite support maximum sigma_i*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ,i> for block i.
  q(u): Nonnegative negative-vertical coordinate of v(u).
  q_rc: The scalar 8+4sqrt(2), twice the first-order coefficient of v_mass.
  r_j: Backward planar state for a local finite list of nonnegative half-steps, with r_N=(epsilon,-1) and r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha_j*v_(j-1).
  r_m: Specified-minimizer squared-radius multiplier in Section 12, here one.
  rho_m: True closest distance dist(e_0,C_min), proved in(0,1]; the specified minimizer0 has exact radius1.
  sigma_i: Positive local scale 1/(chi*s_i/2+epsilon*b^(i)/2+A).
  t(u): Planar tangent unit vector (-sin u,-cos u).
  t_j: Zero-based physical index 2^j-1 of the first occurrence of level j.
  tau_tail: Exact finite terminal correction (1+2*S_(m-1))/(2*(1+S_(m-1))).
  theta_sp: The spiral endpoint angle arctan(1/epsilon), distinct from the unrelated accepted benchmark symbol Theta.
  v(u): Planar arc point R(u)*e(u)=(p(u),-q(u)).
  v_j: Selected arc projection v(u_j) for r_j in the local backward construction.
  v_mass: The scalar c*(c-2) for the original enclosing-m word.
  xi_j: Exact numerator correction 1/(a*c^j).
  z_circ,i: Local shift (1,0)-sigma_i*(epsilon+P_i,-1-Q_i).
  zeta_i: Minimizer halfspace threshold ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1), where z_circ,i=(c_(i,1),c_(i,2)).
external_refs: []
---

## statement
A static smooth-convex lower witness with an eventual strict benchmark margin for the prescribed original ruler family.

All vector spaces, norms and inner products below are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. Log denotes the natural logarithm. Set lambda=1+sqrt(2). For each integer m>=6 put beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c, and define H_0=() and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j) for integers 0<=j<m. Every child uses these same enclosing-m parameters. Write n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j and b_j=1+a*c^j. The step word under consideration is exactly H_m.

For nonnegative real x,y define Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2 and G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2. Retain the original ALL-split benchmark C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1, C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)) and B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)), with each split index r an integer. Put U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Let Z_1=lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m, whose existence, positivity and finiteness are unconditional conclusions of predecessor fact 04219554e41d9750.

Use exactly K=14/5, E=1/100, kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m and the following costs:

    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]-1,
    A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon,
    R_cut=max{A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2},
    J=max{1,ceil(log(R_cut/a)/log c)}.

The unsubscripted H in this display is a scalar allowance, distinct from the words H_j. If J<m, let k=m-J and select only the first occurrences of levels J,J+1,...,m-1, at physical zero-based step indices 2^(J+i)-1 for 0<=i<k. Their values are b^(i)=b_(J+i). Set s_0=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-1) for 1<=i<k, and s_k=S_(m-1). These are the sums of the actual incoming ordinary blocks and terminal ordinary block, with every unselected step retained. Define

    P_m = 1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
          * product_(i=0)^(k-1)
              [b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i))]^2.

If J>=m, set k=0 and P_m=1/[2*(1+S_m)]. Define the constant

    L_joint=Z_1/(2*lambda)
       *exp[-1+(8+4sqrt(2)-14*pi/5-1/50)
                    *(1-5*pi/(28*log lambda))].

For any nonnegative integer n and any finite nonnegative word h of length n, define W_n(h) as the supremum of 2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2) over all finite dimensions, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, under actual gradient descent x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). Here L>0 is fixed and arbitrary; this is the W=2R normalization.

There exists a finite integer M>=6 such that J<m for every integer m>=M and the following holds for EVERY such m and EVERY L>0. The explicit construction in the proof gives a single static function F_m:R^(m-J+1)->R, independent of L, such that f_m=L*F_m is convex and differentiable, has L-Lipschitz gradient, and satisfies f_m(0)=min f_m=0. Starting at the standard unit vector e_0, actual gradient descent uses EVERY step of H_m in its original physical order, and its exact payoff about the specified actual minimizer 0 is

    2*(f_m(x_(n_m))-f_m(0))/(L*||e_0-0||^2)
        =P_m>(1+1/1000)*U_(n_m)^ZJ.

The specified radius is exactly ||e_0||=1. For this same function, let C_min=argmin f_m and rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min). The minimum set is the explicit nonempty closed convex polyhedron derived in the proof, and 0<rho_m<=1. The payoff about the closest minimizer is exactly P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. No exact limit for that different normalization is asserted.

All actual query points, the terminal point, and any minimizer satisfy EVERY ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequality: if f_u=f_m(x_u) and g_u=grad f_m(x_u), then for every ordered pair u,v,

    f_u-f_v+<g_u,x_v-x_u>+||g_u-g_v||^2/(2L)<=0.

Their Gram matrices are positive semidefinite because they are actual Euclidean vectors. The exact limit about the specified minimizer and the resulting worst-case lower bound are

    lim_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*P_m
        =L_joint>5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000,
    liminf_(m->infinity) B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)
        >=L_joint>1001/1000.

Only the unconditional original benchmark/ray conclusions and certified saved-endpoint scope of fact 04219554e41d9750 are used as accepted input. This is a lower witness for ONE prescribed family. It asserts no minimax counterexample, no optimality over schedules or checkpoint selections, no exact closest-radius limit, no numerical value of M, and no resolution of the original all-n/all-nonnegative-word problem.

## proof
# A static smooth-convex witness above the benchmark on the original ruler

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL CANDIDATE; UNVERIFIED.
Worker max4, round58. Assignment TASK.md; steering60da2cf01388bce5.
This is a prescribed-word lower witness, not a minimax resolution.

The only accepted mathematical input is fact04219554e41d9750, restricted
to its ORIGINAL benchmark ray and certified rational enclosure. The two
unverified source drafts c8fcf4ced5a34046 and8880a32b8502cf2f are provenance,
not predecessors: all geometry and scalar arguments needed here are
derived together below. No mathematical program, numerical evaluation,
parameter scan, executed recurrence, solver, formal gate, installation,
or sub-agent is used. Source originals are preserved.

## 1. Definitions and quantities to be realized

Set lambda=1+sqrt(2). For each integer m>=6 use exactly

    beta=1-1/m, c=lambda*(1+1/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    n_m=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, b_j=1+a*c^j.

Every child retains the SAME enclosing m. Only tau=1 is used anywhere
in this theorem. The finite lower bound below is proved whenever the
selected set is nonempty; this holds for every sufficiently large m.

Retain the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark:

    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4*x*y))/2,
    G(x,y)=2*x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8*x*y)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) G(C_j,B_(n-j-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Every split index is an integer. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives

    Z_1=lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m >0,
    lambda^(-r)*B_(2^r-1) <= Z_1
      <=lambda^(-r)*(sqrt(B_(2^r))+3/100)^2, r>=0.       (1)

Define the EXACT geometric parameters and costs

    K=14/5, E=1/100, kappa=K/m, epsilon=E/m,
    theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon),
    chi=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp), d=1-kappa^2,
    T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-1)/d,
    H=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]-1,
    A=epsilon+(H+1)/epsilon.                           (2)

Here 0<kappa<1 and 0<epsilon<=1/2. The symbol H without a subscript
denotes the geometric vertical allowance, not a physical word H_j.
Use the frozen selection rule, without altering any finite threshold:

    R_cut=max{A/epsilon,1+2/epsilon^2},
    J=max{1,ceil(log(R_cut/a)/log c)}.                  (3)

If J>=m the selection is empty and k=0. Otherwise select every FIRST
occurrence of the levels J,J+1,...,m-1 and no other physical step, and
put k=m-J.
Checkpoint i, 0<=i<k, has level J+i and value b^(i)=b_(J+i).
Its incoming ordinary word is denoted by Aword_i and its sum by s_i.
The terminal ordinary word is Aword_k, with sum s_k. In the empty case
Aword_0=H_m and s_0=S_m.

For the nonempty case define the scalar value to be realized:

    P_m = 1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
          * product_(i=0)^(k-1)
              [b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i))]^2.   (4)

For the empty case define P_m=1/[2*(1+S_m)].

Write q_rc=8+4sqrt(2) and theta=5*pi/(28*log lambda), and define

    L_joint=Z_1/(2*lambda)
         *exp[-1+(q_rc-14*pi/5-1/50)*(1-theta)].        (5)

The original normalized worst-case error is

    W_n(h)=sup 2*(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L*||x_0-x_star||^2), (6)

over all finite Euclidean dimensions, convex differentiable functions
with L-Lipschitz gradient and an attained minimum x_star, and x_0!=x_star,
under actual GD x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t). The final theorem appears
after its supporting construction and estimates. It concerns only this
prescribed family; the original minimax and uniform all-word target
remain open.

## 2. Original physical indices, sums and the paid threshold

Put zeta=a/(c-2)=beta/[c*(c-2)] and v_mass=c*(c-2). For x=1/m,

    v_mass=1+(4+2sqrt(2))*x+lambda^2*x^2>1>beta>0,
    0<zeta<1,
    S_j=zeta*c^j+(1-zeta)*2^j-1, 0<=j<=m.            (7)

Indeed S_0=0. Substitution into S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j gives the
next identity because zeta*(c-2)=a. This proves (7) by induction.

Index physical steps from0. The first occurrence of b_j is at
t_j=2^j-1. The prefix before it is H_j; for j<m-1 the word between
t_j and t_(j+1) is H_j; after t_(m-1) is H_(m-1). These statements follow inductively
from the displayed concatenation, preserving its fixed enclosing m.
Thus for J<m the decomposition is exactly

    H_J, b_J, H_J, b_(J+1), H_(J+1), b_(J+2), ...,
                    H_(m-2), b_(m-1), H_(m-1).        (8)

In particular s_0=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-1) for1<=i<k, and s_k=S_(m-1).
There are k original selected steps and n_m-k ordinary steps. All
unselected copies remain in their actual physical positions.

From (3), a*c^J>=R_cut. Therefore EVERY selected b^(i) satisfies

    b^(i)>=2*(1+epsilon^(-2)),
    epsilon*b^(i)>=epsilon+A>A.                       (9)

The doubled source-format threshold is met at finite m, not merely
asymptotically. It also implies the smaller one-envelope threshold.

## 3. Independent-parameter arc and finite backward trajectories

We prove the local construction for arbitrary0<epsilon<=1/2 and
0<kappa<1, with the exact costs (2). No ordering kappa<=epsilon is used.
For0<=u<=theta_sp define

    e(u)=(cos u,-sin u), t(u)=(-sin u,-cos u),
    R(u)=chi*exp(-kappa*u),
    v(u)=R(u)*e(u)=(p(u),-q(u)), n(u)=e(u)+kappa*t(u).

Let K_arc be the convex hull of0 and this compact arc. Its endpoints
are v(0)=(chi,0) and v(theta_sp)=(epsilon,-1). Differentiation gives
p'=-R*(kappa*cos u+sin u)<0 and q'=R*(cos u-kappa*sin u). Therefore

    epsilon<=p(u)<=chi, q(u)>=0, q(u)/p(u)<=1/epsilon.  (10)

In particular q is allowed to exceed1. For w-u=Delta,

    <n(u),v(w)>/R(u)
       =exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta).

The derivative in Delta is
-(1+kappa^2)*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta. On the entire possible
interval [-theta_sp,theta_sp], the expression has maximum1 at0. Also
<n(u),0>=0<R(u). Thus n(u) supports K_arc at v(u). At v(0), the
additional vector (0,1) supports K_arc because q>=0.

Take ANY finite ordered list alpha_1,...,alpha_N>=0. Define backward
vectors r_j and selected arc points v_j as follows. Start with
r_N=v_N=(epsilon,-1) and angle u_N=theta_sp. Suppose r_j=(U_j,-Y_j)
is defined. With alpha=alpha_j, if

    Y_j<=kappa*[U_j+(alpha-1)*chi],                   (11)

set v_(j-1)=v(0), u_(j-1)=0, r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(0).
Otherwise use the unique u in(0,u_j] satisfying

    <r_j,e(u)-t(u)/kappa>/R(u)=1-alpha,              (12)

and set u_(j-1)=u, v_(j-1)=v(u), r_(j-1)=r_j+alpha*v(u).
These are exact definitions, not executed root searches.

Here is existence, uniqueness and the projection proof. Inductively

    U_j>=epsilon, Y_j>=1, Y_j/U_j<=1/epsilon;           (13)

at every uncapped point there is a_j>=0 with

    r_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*e(u_j)+kappa*a_j*t(u_j)].       (14)

Initially a_N=0. In (11) let mu=U_j+(alpha-1)*chi. Then mu>0 and
r_(j-1)-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-Y_j). Both are nonnegative
supporting-normal combinations, proving projection to v(0). The
inequality becomes Y_(j-1)<=kappa*(U_(j-1)-chi), so all earlier
backward entries are capped too.

If (11) fails, the point is not already capped. The left side of
(12) is1 at u_j by (14), less than1-alpha at0, and has derivative

    (kappa+kappa^(-1))*(U_j*cos u+Y_j*sin u)/R(u)>0.

This gives exactly one root, including alpha=0 with root u_j.
Representation (14) shows that the polar angle of r_j is at least
u_j, so <r_j,t(u)>=0 at that root. Equation (12) says that
r_j+(alpha-1)*v(u) is a nonnegative multiple of n(u): its t-component
is kappa times its e-component. This proves (14) for j-1 and the
projection property. Adding alpha*(p,-q) preserves (13) by (10).
Finally, if x-v has nonpositive dot product with every w-v in K_arc,
then ||x-w||^2>=||x-v||^2; thus all the supporting-normal arguments
indeed prove v_j=Pi_(K_arc)(r_j).

Define

    P=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*p(u_(j-1)),
    Q=sum_(j=1)^N alpha_j*q(u_(j-1)), S=sum_j alpha_j.

Then r_0=(epsilon+P,-1-Q), 0<=P<=chi*S and P>=epsilon*Q.
To bound Q independently of the word, consider two consecutive
uncapped angles u_old<=u_new and Delta=u_new-u_old. Taking the
t(u_old) component of r_old=r_new+alpha*v(u_old) gives exactly

    a_old=B_Delta+A_Delta*a_new,
    A_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*(cos Delta+sin Delta/kappa),
    B_Delta=exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta/kappa.          (15)

Both coefficients are nonnegative. With d=1-kappa^2>0,

    A_Delta'-(d/kappa)*A_Delta
       =-(1+kappa^(-2))*exp(-kappa*Delta)*sin Delta<=0.

Hence A_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa), while B_Delta<=Delta/kappa and
1+d*B_Delta<=exp(d*Delta/kappa). It follows that
1+d*a_old<=exp(d*Delta/kappa)*(1+d*a_new). Telescoping from a_N=0
gives a_j<=T. By (14),

    Y_j=R(u_j)*[(1+a_j)*sin u_j+kappa*a_j*cos u_j]
       <=chi*[1+(1+kappa)*T]=1+H.

A cap leaves Y unchanged. Consequently

    0<=Q<=H, 0<=P<=chi*S, P>=epsilon*Q.               (16)

The needed geometry has now been proved without the false restriction
q<=1, without nonnegative horizontal normals, and without any per-step
unpriced error. The half-sum assembly below uses physical zero regions;
it does not require bounds on horizontal proximal coordinates.

## 4. Finite local functions and their zero regions

For a physical ordinary block of sum s and its physical checkpoint b,
apply Section3 to its entries divided by2. Write N for its length and
P,Q,r_j,v_j for that HALF-step construction. Set b_loc=b/2 and

    sigma=1/(chi*s/2+epsilon*b/2+A),
    z_circ=(1,0)-sigma*(epsilon+P,-1-Q),
    U=1-sigma*(P+epsilon*b_loc), Y=sigma*Q,
    V_fin={0,v(0),v_0,...,v_N},
    phi(z)=sigma*max_(v in V_fin)<v,z-z_circ>,
    f_loc(x)=min_z [phi(z)+||x-z||^2/2].              (17)

By (16), P<=chi*s/2 and Q<=H. Thus U>=0 and

    sigma*[P+epsilon+(1+Q)/epsilon]<=1.               (18)

The function phi is a finite nonnegative convex maximum of affine
functions. The minimizing objective defining f_loc is coercive and
strictly convex, so its proximal point z exists uniquely. If g=x-z,
the minimizing condition is g in partial phi(z). To justify both
directions directly, this subgradient inequality makes the cost at
any other w at least its cost at z plus ||w-z||^2/2. Conversely,
optimality at z, tested against z+t*(w-z) for0<t<1, and convexity of
phi imply phi(w)-phi(z)>=<g,w-z>-t*||w-z||^2/2. Letting t decrease
to0 proves the subgradient inequality. For two such pairs,
the two subgradient inequalities imply
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>. Moreover using z as trial point at x+w gives

    f_loc(x+w)<=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w||^2/2.

Using the actual new proximal point z' and the subgradient inequality
at z gives

    f_loc(x+w)>=f_loc(x)+<g,w>+||w-(z'-z)||^2/2.

These bounds prove differentiability with gradient g, global convexity,
and a1-Lipschitz gradient. Also f_loc>=0 and {f_loc=0}={phi=0}.

At x_j=z_circ+sigma*r_j, put z_j=x_j-sigma*v_j. Since the convex hull
of V_fin is contained in K_arc and includes v_j, its projection
inequality implies that sigma*v_j is an active subgradient of phi at
z_j. Thus grad f_loc(x_j)=sigma*v_j. In particular the original
ordered half-steps run exactly from x_0=(1,0) to
(U+sigma*epsilon*b_loc,Y); the step b_loc then reaches
(U,Y+sigma*b_loc).

For every nonzero v=(p,-q) in V_fin, (10) and (18) give

    <v,-z_circ>
      =-p+sigma*[p*(epsilon+P)+q*(1+Q)]<=0.

Therefore phi=f_loc=0 on {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}. Also b_loc>=1+epsilon^(-2)
by (9), so

    <v,(U,Y)-z_circ>=sigma*[(1-b_loc)*epsilon*p+q]<=0.

Increasing the second coordinate decreases this inner product. Hence

    f_loc(U,y)=0 for every y>=Y.                      (19)

Along the actual local path the first coordinate decreases and the
second increases. Before its checkpoint they obey x_1>=U and
0<=x_2<=Y. These are physical coordinate statements, valid independently
of the sign of the supporting normal. Zero steps, if present, retain
their original index and cause no exception to the construction.

## 5. One finite-dimensional static function and every physical update

First suppose J<m. Apply Section4 separately in advance to each actual
incoming block Aword_i and its checkpoint b^(i), i=0,...,k-1. All its symbols receive
the subscript i: sigma_i,P_i,Q_i,phi_i,f_i,U_i,Y_i,z_circ,i.
Define positive scales and nonnegative offsets by

    D_0=1, ell_0=0,
    G_i=sigma_i*b^(i)/2
       =b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)),
    D_(i+1)=D_i*G_i,
    ell_(i+1)=D_i*Y_i,
    delta_tail=D_k/(1+s_k).                           (20)

The basis e_0,...,e_k is the standard orthonormal basis of R^(k+1).
For any delta>0 define the one-sided Huber function

    Hub_delta(z)=0                     if z<=0,
                 z^2/2                 if0<=z<=delta,
                 delta*z-delta^2/2      if z>=delta.

Its continuous derivative is max{0,min{z,delta}}, which is nondecreasing
and1-Lipschitz. The ACTUAL function used in the theorem is

    F_m(x)=(1/2)*sum_(i=0)^(k-1) D_i^2
                    *f_i((x_i-ell_i)/D_i,x_(i+1)/D_i)
           +(1/2)*Hub_(delta_tail)(x_k-ell_k).         (21)

This is a single function fixed before running GD. The finite lists
of planes, implicit backward roots, scales and shifts are mathematical
definitions determined by the entire prescribed word. No trajectory
is replayed or numerically solved to define (21). Its dimension is
exactly k+1=m-J+1 in this nonempty case, finite at every m. Each local
maximum has only finitely many planes even though the auxiliary arc is continuous.

The function is nonnegative and convex. Every ell_i>=0, so at x=0
each local input is in {z_1<=0,z_2>=0}, and the terminal term vanishes.
Therefore F_m(0)=min F_m=0. Each scaled local term before its factor1/2
has the1-smooth upper inequality on its two coordinates. Summing,

    F_m(x+w)<=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>
          +(1/4)*[sum_(i<k)(w_i^2+w_(i+1)^2)+w_k^2]
       <=F_m(x)+<grad F_m(x),w>+||w||^2/2.             (22)

Every coordinate is counted at most twice. This pays the overlap cost
once in the explicit coefficient1/2; no dimension factor remains.
For completeness, (22) implies1-Lipschitz gradient here: fix y and
apply (22) to V(x)=F_m(x)-<grad F_m(y),x> with w=-grad V(x).
Convexity makes y a minimizer of V. Consequently

    F_m(x)-F_m(y)-<grad F_m(y),x-y>
       >=||grad F_m(x)-grad F_m(y)||^2/2.              (23)

Add the reversed inequality and apply Cauchy--Schwarz to obtain the
Lipschitz bound. This argument does not assume a least smoothness
constant or an extra rescaling of the steps.

At the start of block i the claimed physical state is

    x_j=ell_j+D_j*U_j for j<i,
    x_i=ell_i+D_i,
    x_j=0 for j>i.                                    (24)

It is true initially at e_0. Follow the scaled local path in coordinates
i,i+1, leaving the other coordinates fixed. Before its checkpoint,

    x_i>=ell_i+D_i*U_i>=ell_i,
    0<=x_(i+1)<=D_i*Y_i=ell_(i+1).

For a previous component j<i, its first input is U_j and its second
is at least ell_(j+1)/D_j=Y_j. Thus (19) makes that component zero.
For a future component j>i, its first input is nonpositive and its
second input is0, so the other zero region makes it zero. The terminal
term is also zero until its activation. A differentiable nonnegative
function has zero gradient at any zero, since every directional
derivative there must vanish. Thus only the current component contributes.

Its global gradient is D_i/2 times the local gradient. A physical
step h_t therefore induces exactly the local step h_t/2. This proves
that the candidate local path is the actual GD path of (21) for every
ordinary step, in its given order. The physical checkpoint b^(i) then
leaves

    x_i=ell_i+D_i*U_i,
    x_(i+1)=D_i*(Y_i+sigma_i*b^(i)/2)
           =ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1).

This proves (24) for the next block. No component is removed or changed
at a transition; earlier components remain zero by the same inequality.
All steps, including all unselected occurrences of large levels, have
been included. Empty blocks and zero steps require no deletion.

During the terminal block let u be the sum of its executed physical
steps. Since0<=u<=s_k, its shifted moving coordinate is

    x_k-ell_k=D_k-delta_tail*u/2
       >=D_k-delta_tail*s_k/2
        =delta_tail*(1+s_k/2)>=delta_tail.            (25)

Every earlier component remains zero. The actual gradient equals
(delta_tail/2)*e_k at every terminal query and at the terminal point.
Hence direct substitution into (21) gives

    F_m(x_(n_m))=D_k^2/[4*(1+s_k)].                   (26)

Equations (20), (26), s_k=S_(m-1), and ||e_0-0||=1 prove EXACTLY (4).
For arbitrary L>0 set f_m=L*F_m. It is L-smooth and GD with physical
steps h_t/L has the identical path, while the normalization cancels L.

For the literal empty-selection case J>=m use k=0,D_0=1,ell_0=0,
and (21) reduces to F_m=Hub_(1/(1+S_m))/2 on R, starting at1.
The same terminal calculation gives normalized payoff1/[2*(1+S_m)].
Thus the exact selection rule also has a defined realization at every
earlier m>=6. No empty-case asymptotic is used in the final theorem.

## 6. All original interpolation conditions and the actual radius

For every ordered pair a,b of the actual query points, the terminal
point, and ANY minimizer, write g_a=grad F_m(x_a), f_a=F_m(x_a).
Inequality (23) with x=x_b,y=x_a yields

    Q_ab=f_a-f_b+<g_a,x_b-x_a>+||g_a-g_b||^2/2<=0.     (27)

Thus every original ordered smooth-convex interpolation row holds,
including both orientations of optimum and terminal rows. It is a
global function inequality, so no untested nonlocal pair or cycle
remains. For f_m=L*F_m the final squared-gradient term is divided by
2L, as required. The update equations were proved in Section5. Any
Gram matrix of these points and gradients is positive semidefinite
because they are actual vectors in R^(k+1): its quadratic form is
the squared norm of their indicated linear combination. Zero steps
give equal actual jets but still retain their physical positions.

The exact minimizer used in (4) is0, already proved to minimize F_m;
its radius is exactly ||e_0||=1. The true closest radius is also
accounted for explicitly. Write z_circ,i=(c_(i,1),c_(i,2)). The zero
set of the local phi_i, and hence f_i, is exactly

    z_1<=c_(i,1),
    epsilon*(z_1-c_(i,1))-(z_2-c_(i,2))<=0.           (28)

The endpoint planes make these inequalities necessary. They suffice
because each other arc slope (p,-q) is

    [(p-epsilon*q)/chi]*(chi,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

a nonnegative combination by (10). Since (21) is a sum of nonnegative
functions, its whole minimum set is the explicit nonempty polyhedron

    C_min={x: x_i<=zeta_i,
                  epsilon*x_i-x_(i+1)<=epsilon*zeta_i-D_i*c_(i,2)
                    for0<=i<k;
               x_k<=ell_k},
    zeta_i=ell_i+D_i*c_(i,1).                         (29)

It contains0. For k>=1 the initial point e_0 violates the first inequality,
because zeta_0=1-sigma_0*(epsilon+P_0)<1. It is therefore outside this
closed minimum set. For k=0 the set is the nonpositive half-line and
the same conclusion holds from initial1. Its exact closest radius is

    rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min), 0<rho_m<=1.                 (30)

Attainment follows by minimizing squared distance on the nonempty
closed set after restricting to a sufficiently large compact ball;
strict convexity makes the projection unique. The radius about the
specified minimizer is1, while the true normalized value is exactly
P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. Thus using the closest minimizer strengthens the final
bound; there is no assumption that0 is the closest minimizer.

## 7. Distinguishing the two geometric formulas and their radius costs

Define, on precisely the same selected positions, the scalar reference

    P5_m=1/(1+2*s_k)*product_(i<k)
               [(b^(i)-1)/(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})]^2. (31)

This is the geometry draft's main mass-only formula(7), and the scalar
draft's F5. In that different one-envelope realization it is a lower
estimate on a payoff normalized by its closest-minimizer radius. Its
finite factors have b-1, a maximum allowance and terminal1/(1+2*s_k).

The ACTUAL function in this document is instead the half-sum (21).
Its specified-minimizer payoff P_m is the geometry draft's formula(8),
equivalently the scalar draft's F5a, with a_sp=2*A/chi. Its factors
have b, the additive allowance2*A and terminal1/[2*(1+s_k)]. Its
specified radius1 and closest radius(30) are properties of this same
actual function, proved above. The one-envelope radius formula from
the other draft is not assigned to (21).

For the remaining calculation (31) is just an explicitly defined
scalar reference. No assertion about the other draft's realization
is used as a premise. In particular one must not replace the ACTUAL
finite value P_m by P5_m. Section9 proves their ratio tends1 with
all accumulated losses paid. The source-format construction itself
has already been derived jointly in Sections3--6.

## 8. The exact activation phase and its logarithmic correction

The cost A in (2) satisfies the useful exact identity

    A=epsilon+sqrt(1+epsilon^2)/[epsilon*(1-kappa)]
           *[exp(theta_sp/kappa)-kappa*exp(kappa*theta_sp)]. (32)

Indeed 1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)-kappa)/(1-kappa),
and chi*exp(d*theta_sp/kappa)=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(theta_sp/kappa).

With fixed K=14/5,E=1/100, the integral defining arctan gives
theta_sp=pi/2-E/m+O(m^(-3)). Factoring exp(theta_sp/kappa) from the bracket
in (32), its relative subtracted term is exponentially small in m.
The separate additive epsilon is also exponentially smaller than the
remaining positive term. The logarithms of sqrt(1+epsilon^2) and
1/(1-kappa) have bounded Taylor remainders of orders m^(-2) and m^(-1).
It follows, with constants independent of m, that

    log A=pi*m/(2*K)+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m),
    log(A/epsilon)=pi*m/(2*K)+2*log m-2*log E-E/K+O(1/m). (33)

For explicit remainder justification, the arctan integral differs
from epsilon by at most epsilon^3/3; the log expansion follows from
integrating1/(1+t) on a fixed interval about0. After some fixed m,
theta_sp is bounded below by a positive constant, so the exponential
relative remainders are bounded by a fixed multiple of exp(-c_0*m)
for some c_0>0. Taking their logarithms is then justified by the
same integral bound. These estimates require no evaluated exponential.

The other quantity1+2/epsilon^2 in R_cut has polynomial growth, so
R_cut=A/epsilon for every sufficiently large m. Since
log c=log lambda+1/m+O(m^(-2)) and a->1/lambda, the exact ceiling gives

    J=theta*m+(2/log lambda)*log m+O(1),
    theta=pi/(2*K*log lambda)=5*pi/(28*log lambda).    (34)

The O(1) includes the exact rounding in (3); it is not an alteration
of the chosen cutoff. Section11 proves0<theta<220/343<1. Hence
1<=J<m for every sufficiently large m and k/m->1-theta>0.
The excluded first theta*m levels and the logarithmic correction are
the activation payment. They are retained in the exponent below.

## 9. Exact products and control of the entire growing index set

For J<m, (8)--(9) give the exact finite identities

    P5_m=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
       *[a*c^J/(chi*S_J+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [a*c^j/(chi*S_(j-1)+epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2,  (35)

    P_m=1/[2*(1+S_(m-1))]
       *[(1+a*c^J)/(chi*S_J+2*A+epsilon*(1+a*c^J))]^2
       *product_(j=J+1)^(m-1)
          [(1+a*c^j)/(chi*S_(j-1)+2*A
                                    +epsilon*(1+a*c^j))]^2. (36)

The maximum in (31) is EXACTLY epsilon*b^(i), by (9). Products with
no factors are1. In particular a single selected checkpoint retains
the same prefix and tail H_(m-1).

Define the exact scalar corrections

    delta_l=[(1-zeta)*2^l-1]/(zeta*c^l),
    xi_j=1/(a*c^j),
    D_first=chi*(1+delta_J)+epsilon*(c-2)*(1+xi_J),
    D_bulk,j=chi*(1+delta_(j-1))+epsilon*v_mass*(1+xi_j),
    d_first=chi+epsilon*(c-2), d_bulk=chi+epsilon*v_mass.

The denominators D_first,D_bulk,j in this section are scalar
normalizations, distinct from the physical scales D_i in Section5.
Using a/zeta=c-2 and a*c/zeta=v_mass, (35) becomes

    P5_m=(c-2)^2*v_mass^[2*(k-1)]
       /[(1+2*S_(m-1))*D_first^2*product_(j>J)D_bulk,j^2]. (37)

The exceptional first factor is c-2, not v_mass. Replacing its prefix
by H_(J-1) would introduce an erroneous factor tending to lambda^2.

Here c stays in a compact subset of(2,infinity), a and zeta are bounded
away from0, and1-zeta=O(1/m). The EXACT errors obey

    sum_(l>=J)|delta_l|
      <=[(1-zeta)/zeta]*(2/c)^J/(1-2/c)
                         +(1/zeta)*c^(-J)/(1-1/c),
    sum_(j>=J)xi_j=c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)].                (38)

These sums extend to infinity only to upper-bound nonnegative error
terms; no infinite-dimensional function is constructed. By (34) the
bounds vanish exponentially in m. Since d_first,d_bulk>=chi>=1,

    |D_first/d_first-1|<=|delta_J|+epsilon*(c-2)*xi_J,
    |D_bulk,j/d_bulk-1|<=|delta_(j-1)|+epsilon*v_mass*xi_j.

The sum of these bounds, with delta_J counted twice if necessary,
vanishes by (38). Each is eventually at most1/2. The integral formula
for log gives |log(1+t)|<=2|t| there. Therefore replacing all normalized
denominators simultaneously by d_first,d_bulk incurs a total logarithmic
error tending0, explicitly controlled by (38). This is a bound on the
whole product, not a pointwise o(1) multiplied by k without control.

It remains to pay every difference between the actual P_m and P5_m.
For each selected level j, let s^(j)=S_J when j=J and S_(j-1) otherwise,
and put

    V_j=2*A/(chi*s^(j)+epsilon*b_j),
    tau_tail=(1+2*S_(m-1))/[2*(1+S_(m-1))] in(0,1).

The exact ratio is

    P_m/P5_m=tau_tail
                   *product_(j=J)^(m-1)[(1+xi_j)/(1+V_j)]^2. (39)

For every sufficiently large m, (38) gives S_l>=zeta*c^l/2 for l>=J.
The EXACT cutoff (3) gives A*c^(-J)<=epsilon*a. Thus

    sum_j V_j
      <=[4*A/(zeta*chi)]*c^(-J)*[1+1/(1-1/c)]
      <=[4*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)].           (40)

The separate1 in the bracket pays the first prefix; the geometric
series pays every later gap, including the repeated H_J. Since
-log tau_tail=log[1+1/(1+2*S_(m-1))]<=1/(1+2*S_(m-1)) and
0<=log(1+t)<=t for t>=0, (38)--(40) give

    |log(P_m/P5_m)|
      <=1/(1+2*S_(m-1))
        +2*c^(-J)/[a*(1-1/c)]
        +[8*epsilon*(c-2)/chi]*[1+1/(1-1/c)] ->0.      (41)

This pays the source-format additive2*A, b versus b-1, and its distinct
terminal factor. Its last term is O(1/m); the other terms decay
exponentially. No checkpoint loss is silently dropped.

## 10. The exact limit, with the first record and terminal block retained

Set gamma=4+2sqrt(2), q_rc=2*gamma. Elementary expansions with uniform
remainders, obtained from the log integral as in Section8, give

    log v_mass=gamma/m+O(m^(-2)),
    log chi=pi*K/(2*m)+O(m^(-2)),
    log(chi+epsilon*v_mass)=(pi*K/2+E)/m+O(m^(-2)).   (42)

The quantities depend only on m, not on the selected level. There
are at most m factors, so summing all O(m^(-2)) remainders gives
O(1/m). Equations (37)--(38) therefore give

    P5_m=(c-2)^2/[(1+2*S_(m-1))*d_first^2]
                 *[v_mass/d_bulk]^[2*(k-1)]*exp(o(1)). (43)

The o(1) is bounded by the explicit sums in (38). Moreover
d_first->1, c-2->lambda^(-1), zeta->1, and (7) gives

    lambda^m/(1+2*S_(m-1)) -> lambda*exp(-1)/2.        (44)

To verify (44), divide S_(m-1) by zeta*c^(m-1); its error delta_(m-1)
tends0 by (38). Then use (lambda/c)^m=(1+1/m)^(-m)->exp(-1).
The logarithm of the growing power in (43) tends, by (34),(42), to

    (q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta).                       (45)

The replacement k=(1-theta)*m+O(log m) contributes O(log(m)/m) to
this logarithm. Combined with (1), (41), and (43)--(45), this proves

    lim B_(n_m)*P_m
      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+(q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta)]
      =L_joint.                                     (46)

Every geometric/product error has been summed: it is O(log(m)/m)
apart from exponentially decaying terms before applying the accepted
benchmark convergence. Only the already accepted convergence in (1)
is needed, so no new convergence rate for B_n is assumed or computed.
The factor1/(2*lambda) includes the exceptional first record and the
true terminal child. The enclosing beta cancels exactly through
a/zeta=c-2; it was not separately approximated in each of k factors.

## 11. An exact strict margin from accepted endpoints and rational bounds

The certified table fixed in Section8 of accepted04219554e41d9750 is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA2562a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Its denominator and saved lower endpoint are

    D80=1208925819614629174706176,
    L1023=19205851554343006584447327614<=D80*B_1023,
    15880*D80<L1023.                                 (47)

This is a read of the accepted saved endpoint, not a new evaluation
of the benchmark recurrence or its certificate. The table hash was
matched; no numerical or exact recurrence was executed.

Squaring gives140/99<sqrt(2)<99/70, since19600<19602 and9800<9801.
Successive multiplication gives lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)<6727.
Thus (1) at r=10 and (47) yield

    Z_1>15880/6727>59/25,                             (48)

where15880*25=397000>396893=6727*59. Also lambda<17/7, since
sqrt(2)<10/7. Hence

    Z_1/(2*lambda)>413/850.                           (49)

We require no upper estimate for Z_1. For the remaining constants,
polynomial division and integration give

    22/7-pi=integral_0^1 x^4*(1-x)^4/(1+x^2) dx>0.

Set t=(lambda-1)/(lambda+1)=sqrt(2)-1>41/99. Integrating the first
two nonnegative terms of1/(1-t^2) gives

    log lambda=2*integral_0^t du/(1-u^2)
      >2*(t+t^3/3)>2548888/2910897>7/8,
    8*2548888-7*2910897=14825>0.                      (50)

Consequently0<theta=5*pi/(28*log lambda)<220/343, as used in (34),
and1-theta>123/343. With K=14/5,E=1/100,

    q_rc-pi*K-2*E
      >8+560/99-44/5-1/50=23941/4950>0.

Therefore the exponent in (46) satisfies

    -1+(q_rc-pi*K-2*E)*(1-theta)
      >-1+(23941/4950)*(123/343)
       =1246893/1697850>73/100.                      (51)

Positive terms of the exponential series, used as a textual inequality,
give

    exp(73/100)>1+73/100+(73/100)^2/2+(73/100)^3/6
               =12367717/6000000.

Combining with (49),

    L_joint>5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.           (52)

The final comparison is exact: on denominator5100000000, subtracting
1001/1000 leaves numerator2767121>0. All arithmetic in this section
is a displayed symbolic proof using an already accepted endpoint;
no arithmetic program or numerical evaluation was run.

## 12. Complete match to every scalar-interface condition

This section spells out the interface from Section9 of the scalar
source as a consequence of the JOINT proof, not as a conditional arrow.

| Required item | Actual item proved here |
| --- | --- |
| Original word and indices | tau=1, same m in every child, n_m=2^m-1, t_j=2^j-1; (7)--(9) |
| One static function | f_m=L*F_m from (17),(20),(21), dimension k+1 |
| Effective chi | Exactly (2); m*log chi->7*pi/5 by (42) |
| Effective epsilon | Exactly1/(100*m), without an added tilt coefficient |
| Effective activation A | Exactly (2),(32); log A=(5*pi/28)*m+log m-log E-E/K+O(1/m) |
| Physical threshold | Exactly2*(1+10000*m^2), met by (9) |
| Exact selection cutoff | Exactly (3), including its maximum, ceiling, minimum level1 and empty case |
| Actual transfers | D_(i+1)=D_i*b^(i)/(chi*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b^(i)), (20) |
| Actual terminal factor | 2*F_m(x_n)=D_k^2/[2*(1+S_(m-1))], (26) |
| All accumulated scalar differences | Explicit bounds (38),(40),(41),(42); no pointwise-only error |
| Smoothness and overlap | L-smooth by (22)--(23); coefficient1/2 paid in local half-steps and terminal factor |
| Minimum and initial radius | Actual minimizer0, x_0=e_0, specified radius1; true rho_m in(0,1], (29)--(30) |
| All original interpolation conditions | Actual GD and every ordered row (27), real PSD Gram |

The source-format alternative in that Section9 is satisfied with exact
transfers, its exact terminal factor, radius multiplier r_m=1 and no
additional per-transfer factor. Thus its limiting loss is ell_loss=0.
The costs are C_eff=7*pi/5, alpha_eff=5*pi/28 and
theta=alpha_eff/log lambda. The threshold grows polynomially, so it
meets the required activation exponent. The sufficient radius/loss
budget is r*exp(2*ell_loss)=1<1001/1000, where r=lim r_m=1.

One can also match its PRIMARY conditions G2--G3 exactly, rather than
confusing the two finite terminal factors. Let tau_tail,V_j be those
in (39)--(40). On the same physical trajectory define bookkeeping
scales hatD_i=D_i for i<k and hatD_k=sqrt(tau_tail)*D_k. Then

    2*F_m(x_(n_m))=hatD_k^2/(1+2*S_(m-1)).            (53)

For i<k-1 set eta_i=1+V_(J+i), and set
eta_(k-1)=(1+V_(m-1))/sqrt(tau_tail). They are all at least1.
Because b_j=(b_j-1)*(1+xi_j), the exact transfers (20) imply

    hatD_(i+1)>=hatD_i*(b^(i)-1)
       /[(chi*s_i+max{epsilon*b^(i),A})*eta_i].       (54)

This also covers k=1. Every eta is attached to a scale on the actual
path; the final adjustment changes bookkeeping, not the function,
trajectory or word. Equations (40)--(41) give

    0<=sum_i log eta_i
       <=sum_j V_j-(1/2)*log tau_tail ->0.             (55)

Thus G1 is Section5 and (27); G2 is (54)--(55); G3 is (53) with
L restored; G4 is dist(e_0,argmin f_m)^2<=1=hatD_0^2. All four
conditions are proved with the actual costs and accumulated losses.
The source construction is not falsely asserted to dominate P5_m
without these finite corrections.

## 14. Final prescribed-word theorem and eventual strict separation

THEOREM (joint analytical candidate; UNVERIFIED). Let lambda=1+sqrt(2).
For each integer m>1 set beta_m=1-1/m, c_m=lambda*(1+1/m),
a_m=beta_m/c_m, H_0=(), and

    H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_m*c_m^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,

keeping the SAME m in all children; let n_m=2^m-1. Let B_n and U_n^ZJ
be exactly the original all-split benchmark defined in Section1.
Use the fixed geometric parameters kappa_m=14/(5*m),
epsilon_m=1/(100*m), costs (2), and the exact cutoff (3), selecting
only the first-occurrence records at levels J,...,m-1.

There exists a finite integer M such that for EVERY integer m>=M and
EVERY L>0 the function f_m=L*F_m constructed in (17),(20),(21) has all
the following properties. Its finite dimension is m-J+1. It is convex,
differentiable and has L-Lipschitz gradient. It attains its minimum0
at the actual minimizer0. Starting from the explicit unit vector e_0,
actual GD with EVERY step of H_m, in its original physical order and
with steps h_t/L, has normalized terminal error

    2*(f_m(x_(n_m))-f_m(0))/(L*||e_0-0||^2)
       =P_m>(1+1/1000)*U_(n_m)^ZJ.                    (56)

The exact normalized value P_m is (4), and its exact benchmark-scaled
limit is

    lim B_(n_m)*P_m
      =Z_1/(2*lambda)*exp[-1+
          (8+4sqrt(2)-14*pi/5-1/50)
                *(1-5*pi/(28*log lambda))]
      >5107867121/5100000000>1001/1000.               (57)

The closest-minimizer radius is rho_m=dist(e_0,C_min) in(0,1], with
C_min explicitly given by (29). Its true-distance normalized payoff
is P_m/rho_m^2>=P_m. In particular

    liminf B_(n_m)*W_(n_m)(H_m)>=L_joint>1001/1000.     (58)

PROOF. Section8 establishes J<m for all sufficiently large m, with
the exact paid cutoff. Sections3--6 construct the single static
function, prove its claimed smoothness, minimum, both radius statements,
every physical update, every ordered interpolation row, and the exact
payoff (4). Sections9--10 bound all accumulated errors and give (57).
Section11 proves its strict rational lower enclosure. Section12 checks
that no cost is lost in joining the geometry to the scalar estimate.

To pass from the limit to the quantifier in (56), put
eta_margin=(L_joint-1001/1000)/2>0. By (46), after one finite depth
every subsequent m satisfies |B_(n_m)*P_m-L_joint|<eta_margin. Hence
B_(n_m)*P_m>(L_joint+1001/1000)/2>1001/1000. Enlarge that depth to
include m>=6 and J<m. Multiplication by L leaves the path and payoff
unchanged, so this single M works for every L>0. No numerical value
for M is asserted. The closest-radius comparison gives (58).

This completes the assigned prescribed-family derivation as ONE
UNVERIFIED object. It establishes no optimality over checkpoint sets,
no negative resolution of the minimax problem, and no uniform all-n,
all-nonnegative-word affirmative theorem. The original target remains
OPEN; no new verified fact or automatic worker-round renewal is claimed.

## intuition
A finite static sum of locally smoothed spiral support functions transfers the actual prescribed ruler trajectory through consecutive coordinates. The half-sum overlap cost, original physical half-steps, finite activation cutoff, exceptional first block, terminal factor and both radius conventions are all retained. The exact specified-minimizer product has a strictly benchmark-exceeding limit for this one family; the original all-word minimax problem remains open.

---
fact_id: 5e1848ee0b153f5f

author: max3
predecessors: [04219554e41d9750, 4c92d3fc4eb6153e]
glossary_introduces:
  C_pc: Positive certified upper bound on Z_d/Z_(b_pc0) for the chosen positive integer anchor d.
  F_pc_i(t): Certified ratio upper bound U_pc(m*b_pc_i,t)/(a*L_pc(b_pc_i,t)) at nonnegative integer selection stage t.
  H_(i,j): Actual substituted schedule Hsub_(n_(i,j)) of length N_(i,j).
  J: In this phase-cover theorem, the least nonnegative integer with (3/2)^J>=60*b_pc_max/gamma, equal to the displayed logarithmic ceiling.
  J5: Explicit common output depth ceil(log(600000*6^16)/log(3/2)) for the seventeen-phase length-five corollary.
  J5a: Explicit common output depth ceil(log(360000*6^8)/log(3/2)) for the nine-phase anchored length-five corollary.
  J6: Explicit common output depth ceil(log(1200000*7^10)/log(3/2)) for the eleven-phase length-six corollary.
  L_pc(b,j): Certified lower ray endpoint lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1), for positive integer b and nonnegative integer j.
  N_(i,j): Actual physical length m*n_(i,j)=m*b_pc_i*2^j-m.
  P_pc: Finite product upper bound lambda^ell*C_pc.
  U_pc(b,j): Certified upper ray endpoint lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2.
  b_pc0: Positive integer anchor base for the finite multiplicative phase cover.
  b_pc_i: Integer phase base b_pc0*m^i for 0<=i<=K, where m=r+1.
  b_pc_i_star: The phase base b_pc_i evaluated at the index i_star supplied by the finite product argument.
  b_pc_max: Largest candidate phase base b_pc_(K-1)=b_pc0*m^(K-1).
  d_shift: Nonnegative integer offset in the shifted-ray estimate, independent of the positive integer anchor d.
  i_star: A fixed index in {0,...,K-1} whose normalized ratio is at most 1-gamma; certified selection can return another index with ratio below 1-gamma/2.
  n_(i,j): Input tree node count b_pc_i*2^j-1 for phase i and nonnegative integer output depth j.
  q_pc_i(a): Normalized ray ratio Z_(m*b_pc_i)/(a*Z_(b_pc_i)), where a is the locally defined capacity floor.
  q_pc_i_star(a): The normalized ratio q_pc_i(a) evaluated at the fixed index i_star.
  t_pc: Normalized product bound P_pc/a^K in (0,1).
  u_j: Auxiliary error majorant lambda^(-j/2) at nonnegative integer output depth j.
